voxel-grid frustum volumetric sampler: tiles frustum into N×N px voxels, integrates scatter at each step; scene.eevee.volumetric_tile_size='2'|'4'|'8'|'16' (2=densest, 16=fastest, step artefacts at 8+); scene.eevee.volumetric_samples=64 (ray steps per tile); scene.eevee.volumetric_start/end (m) — set End to match scene depth; scene.eevee.use_volumetric_shadows=True: CRITICAL — spot shadow maps occlude the volume outside the cone; without this setting the entire frustum receives scatter uniformly (flat haze, no shaft); scene.eevee.volumetric_shadow_samples=16; Principled Volume shader node: Color=scatter colour, Density=extinction coefficient, Anisotropy=Mie forward-scatter asymmetry (0=isotropic fog, 0.6=visible beam 360°, 0.9=visible only looking toward light); Absorption Colour=black=pure scatter no absorption; World node tree: ShaderNodeVolumePrincipled.Volume→ShaderNodeOutputWorld.Volume; Background shader→Surface sets backdrop; EEVEE Next Bloom: scene.eevee.use_bloom=True; bloom_threshold=0.75 (HDR pixels above this glow); bloom_intensity=0.065 (glow multiplier); bloom_radius=5.5 (spread px); NOTE: early 5.0 builds moved Bloom to Compositor Glare node — if no Render>Bloom section use Compositor instead; fake emission cone for GLB export: EEVEE volumes have no glTF 2.0 representation; build truncated cone mesh (apex at light pos, base at target); material: ShaderNodeMixShader(Fac=0.11, in1=ShaderNodeBsdfTransparent, in2=ShaderNodeEmission); mat.blend_method='BLEND'; cone apex=light_pos, base_radius=tan(half_angle)*length, direction.to_track_quat('Z','Y').to_euler(); in Three.js alpha-blended emissive cone reads as shaft in dark scenes; spot light API: bpy.data.lights.new(name,type='SPOT'); ld.spot_size=full_cone_angle_radians (=half_angle×2); ld.spot_blend=softness; ld.shadow_soft_size=0.04 (small=sharp volumetric shadows); direction.to_track_quat('-Z','Y')=aim spot light -Z axis at target; bmesh cone construction: apex_v=bm.verts.new((0,0,0)); ring=[bm.verts.new((r*cos(2πi/n),r*sin(2πi/n),length)) for i]; cap=bm.verts.new((0,0,length)); side faces [apex,ring[i],ring[(i+1)%n]]; cap faces [cap,ring[(i+1)%n],ring[i]]; | 2026-06-10 | blender-tutorial-physics-cloth-simulation-waving-flag | Physics: Cloth Simulation — Stylised Waving Flag on a Mast (Blender 5.1) | blends/physics/physics-cloth-simulation-waving-flag/ | Blender cloth solver uses implicit Backwards Euler (Baraff & Witkin SIGGRAPH 1998): three spring types per mesh edge — structural (vertex-to-adjacent, resists stretch), shear (vertex-to-diagonal, resists skew), bending (two-hop vertices, resists fold); obj.modifiers.new(name='Cloth',type='CLOTH'); cs.quality=10: sub-steps/frame — prevents coarse-mesh tunnelling; effective timestep=1/(24×quality) — at quality=10 each sub-step≈4.2ms, vertex max travel<2mm per step (well under 6cm mast radius); cs.tension_stiffness=40 N/m + cs.compression_stiffness=40 — high stiffness preserves faceted quad proportions under wind load; cs.shear_stiffness=20; cs.bending_stiffness=5 N·m/rad: lower=soft satin flutter, higher=rigid card-stock; cs.air_damping=1.0: drag coefficient slows free edge under turbulence; pinning: cs.vertex_group_mass='pin_left', cs.pin_stiffness=1.0 — applies restoring spring toward bind-pose location per sub-step (not a rigid constraint — compatible with implicit solver matrix); mod.point_cache.frame_start/end set simulation range; Wind force field: bpy.ops.object.effector_add(type='WIND'); default direction = local -Z; rotation_euler[0]=-pi/2 maps -Z→+Y (perpendicular to XZ-plane flag face); field.noise=2.5: stochastic turbulence via Perlin field per vertex per step — asymmetric loading seeds standing-wave instability; field.flow=0.0: full turbulence, no smoothing; bmesh grid in XZ plane: FLAG_COLS×FLAG_ROWS vertices at x=MAST_RADIUS+col_frac×FLAG_WIDTH, z=FLAG_Z_BOTTOM+row_frac×FLAG_HEIGHT; bm.verts.ensure_lookup_table() required before integer-index access; material split at mid_row: upper=crimson (mat_idx=0), lower=ivory (mat_idx=1); Collision modifier on mast: mast.modifiers.new('Collision',type='COLLISION'); coll.settings.thickness_outer=0.02 — 2cm buffer prevents z-fighting at pin attachment; depsgraph GLB export: scene.frame_set(EXPORT_FRAME) — solver must evaluate all preceding frames in order; dg=evaluated_depsgraph_get(); flag_eval=flag_obj.evaluated_get(dg); bpy.data.meshes.new_from_object(flag_eval,depsgraph=dg) — plain Mesh with no modifier reference; wrap in new object, export GLB, remove — no point-cache bake required, no cache directory on disk; export_apply=True bakes modifier stack; outside sources: Blender Manual Cloth Physics CC-BY-SA 4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/physics/cloth/settings/physics.html; blender-scripting MIT Nicolas Janakiev https://github.com/njanakiev/blender-scripting; glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 Khronos Group https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO |

| 2026-06-10 | blender-tutorial-physics-rigid-body-domino-chain | Physics: Rigid Body Dynamics — Domino Chain Topple (Blender 5.1) | blends/physics/physics-rigid-body-domino-chain/ | bpy.ops.rigidbody.world_add(): creates scene.rigidbody_world; bpy.ops.rigidbody.object_add(type='ACTIVE'|'PASSIVE'): registers object in rbw.collection and creates obj.rigid_body RigidBodyObject; obj.rigid_body is None until object is in rbw.collection — cannot configure before registration; rb.collision_shape='BOX': uses AABB exact for cuboids, O(1) GJK vs CONVEX_HULL O(v) vs MESH (static passive only, BVH); rb.use_margin=True + rb.collision_margin=0.002: Bullet inset shell 2 mm; default 0.04 m (4 cm) causes phantom repulsion for 12 mm thin objects — must be < T/2; rb.friction=0.70: wood-on-wood kinetic coefficient; rb.restitution=0.15: low bounce — wood-floor energy loss; rb.linear_damping=0.04 + rb.angular_damping=0.08: velocity drag prevents infinite sliding/spinning; scene.rigidbody_world.substeps_per_frame=20: divides 41.7 ms frame into 2 ms sub-steps; impact velocity ~2 m/s → 4 mm per sub-step < domino T=12 mm so no tunnelling; scene.rigidbody_world.solver_iterations=20: Projected Gauss-Seidel constraint passes per sub-step; ample for non-stacked bodies; stacking needs 40+; critical tipping angle = arctan(T/H) = arctan(0.012/0.080) = 8.5°; TILT_DEG=12° ensures immediate topple at frame 1 without velocity injection; chain spacing 45 mm (33 mm gap): reach = H - T/2 = 74 mm >> gap → reliable impulse transfer; negative X rotation tips top (+Z) toward +Y (chain direction) per right-hand rule: positive X sends +Z toward -Y so negate; two shared mesh data blocks (mesh_ivory, mesh_navy) for per-colour alternation vs per-object material override (material_slot.link='OBJECT'): shared mesh approach simpler and correct; export_snapshot_glb exports frame-1 state (all standing, first domino tilted); animated export requires bake_to_keyframes first: bpy.ops.rigidbody.bake_to_keyframes(frame_start=1, frame_end=120) converts point-cache to per-object location/rotation keyframes exportable by glTF-Blender-IO; record.py uses EEVEE incremental evaluation — no explicit bake needed for render, same as cloth sim approach; outside sources: Blender Manual Rigid Body CC-BY-SA 4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/physics/rigid_body/index.html; Bullet Physics SDK zlib Erwin Coumans/Google https://github.com/bulletphysics/bullet3; glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 Khronos Group https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO |

output Matrix; matrix multiplication is NOT commutative — M_azim×M_fold folds in local frame first then orients radially (M_fold on right); reversing gives M_fold×M_azim which folds in world frame (all panels tip same direction — wrong for iris); GeometryNodeSetInstanceTransform: SETS (replaces) the instance 4×4 matrix vs Transform Instances which COMPOSES; FunctionNodeAxisAngleToRotation: inputs Axis(VECTOR), Angle(FLOAT, radians); output Rotation(ROTATION socket); per-instance phi field: Index→÷N_PANELS→×2π=phi; fold_angle keyframed via modifier socket identifier from interface.items_tree (not hardcoded index); PANEL_L=0.50m, PANEL_W=0.06m, N=8 petals; panel mesh: GeometryNodeMeshGrid + TransformGeometry(Y=+L/2) to place root edge at local origin; all instances at world origin (Points node), M_i applied by SetInstanceTransform; at theta=pi/2 all tips converge to Z=PANEL_L regardless of phi — mathematical definition of iris close; Compose Matrix defaults (0,0,0) translation + (1,1,1) scale = pure rotation matrix; glTF TRS order Scale→Rotation→Translation column-major; non-uniform shear in composed matrix silently lost at GLB export; keyframe_insert data_path uses socket identifier string not display name; outside sources: Blender Manual Matrix Nodes CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/utilities/matrix/; | 2026-06-11 | blender-tutorial-gn-dual-mesh-voronoi-cell-sphere | GN Dual Mesh — Procedural Voronoi Cell Sphere (Blender 5.1) | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-dual-mesh-voronoi-cell-sphere/ | GeometryNodeDualMesh: topological dual — each input face→output vertex (face centroid), each input vertex→output face (polygon with degree=vertex valence); type identifier 'GeometryNodeDualMesh'; inputs[0]='Mesh'(GEOMETRY); inputs[1]='Keep Boundaries'(BOOL,default=False — set True to preserve boundary edge loop for open surfaces); output='Dual Mesh'(GEOMETRY); REQUIRES triangulated input: quads produce n-gon dual faces where hexagons are expected — always use IcoSphere (pre-triangulated) or GeometryNodeTriangulateMesh first; applied to IcoSphere(subdiv=N): input faces=20×4^N, input vertices=10×4^N+2, output faces=input vertices=10×4^N+2, output vertices=input faces=20×4^N, exactly 12 pentagonal output faces (original 12 ico vertices have valence=5 from Euler's formula: any genus-0 triangulation must have exactly 12 degree-5 vertices for a hexagonal tiling to close — topological consequence of V-E+F=2 and 3F=2E); IcoSphere subdiv=3: 1280 input faces, 642 input vertices → 642 Voronoi output cells; NOISE PERTURBATION BEFORE DUAL: displacing vertices radially (along Normal) changes face-centre positions tangentially, enlarging some output cells and shrinking neighbours — organic irregularity from one scalar parameter (NOISE_STRENGTH=0.08m); ShaderNodeTexNoise with Vector=InputPosition (object-space anchor, pattern stable under object motion); VectorMath(SCALE) scales InputNormal by noise fac × NOISE_STRENGTH; SetPosition offset=Normal×fac×strength; GeometryNodeScaleElements(domain='FACE', scale_mode='UNIFORM'): scales each face toward its OWN centroid when Center socket unconnected (per-element pivot, not global pivot) — CELL_SCALE=0.87 creates 13% inter-cell gap; GeometryNodeExtrudeMesh mode='FACES': Individual Faces=True input socket (BOOL) must be explicitly set; True=per-tile extrusion; False=outer boundary shell (completely different result); EXTRUSION_DEPTH=0.055m; GeometryNodeSetShadeSmooth(shade_smooth=False): flat per-face shading; NOISE_STRENGTH=0.0 recovers geodesic dome dual (12 pentagons + hexagons, fullerene topology); outside sources: Blender Manual Dual Mesh CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/mesh/operations/dual_mesh.html; Sverchok MIT Nikita Gorodetskiy et al. https://github.com/nortikin/sverchok; geometry-processing-js MIT Geometry Processing Group Columbia https://github.com/geometryprocessing/geometry-processing-js |

| 2026-06-11 | blender-tutorial-shader-procedural-lava-magma-flow | Shader — Procedural Lava / Magma Flow Material (Blender 5.1) | blends/shading/shader-procedural-lava-magma-flow/ | ShaderNodeTexVoronoi feature='DISTANCE_TO_EDGE': returns 0.0 at every Voronoi cell boundary, rises toward 1.0 at each cell centre; INVERT (1−dist) → 1.0 at crack edges = bright emission, 0.0 at plate centres = dark; feature='F1' (distance to nearest cell centre): 0.0 at centres, rises toward 1.0 at edges; used for BOTH plate colour ramp and displacement (invert F1 → 1.0 at centre = elevated plate, 0.0 at edge = sunken groove); critical distinction: DISTANCE_TO_EDGE=0 at BOUNDARIES (crack seams), F1=0 at CENTRES (plate hearts) — mixing these inverts the visual (glowing plates, dark cracks); Material displacement requires mat.displacement_method='DISPLACEMENT' (Python) or Material Properties▸Settings▸Surface▸Displacement='Displacement and Bump' in UI — without this, Displacement node output is silently ignored; ShaderNodeDisplacement space='WORLD' (not OBJECT — object-space displacement changes with scale transforms, world-space is size-stable); Subdivision Surface modifier type='SIMPLE' levels=4 — preserves flat topology and lets displacement sculpt it; SIMPLE vs CATMULL_CLARK: CATMULL_CLARK rounds corners before displacement (distorts plate boundaries); EEVEE Next Bloom: scene.eevee.use_bloom=True, threshold=1.0; emission strength=6.0 triggers halation at threshold; Bloom moved from Compositor post-process (Blender 3.x) to render effect (4.x+); Mapping node Y Location driver: expression='frame*0.003' — frame is a built-in driver variable, NOT bpy.context.scene.frame_current (which freezes at script execution time); both Voronoi nodes share same Mapping output so cracks and plates scroll in lockstep; KHR_materials_emissive_strength: glTF exporter auto-applies extension when Emission.Strength>1.0; without extension per-channel cap=1.0 clips bright cracks; glTF-Blender-IO (Apache-2.0, bundled) sets emissiveStrength JSON field automatically; outside sources: Blender Manual Voronoi Texture CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/shader_nodes/textures/voronoi.html; KHR_materials_emissive_strength Apache-2.0 Khronos Group https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/tree/main/extensions/2.0/Khronos/KHR_materials_emissive_strength |

| 2026-06-11 | blender-tutorial-sculpt-dyntopo-voxel-remesh | Sculpt Mode — Dynamic Topology + Voxel Remesh: Organic Form (Blender 5.1) | blends/sculpting/sculpt-dyntopo-voxel-remesh/ | Dyntopo (Dynamic Topology): bpy.context.scene.tool_settings.sculpt.use_dynamic_topology_remeshing=True — only valid while context is Sculpt Mode; detail_type_method options: 'RELATIVE'(scales to bounding box), 'CONSTANT'(uniform pixel density, recommended for character heads), 'BRUSH'(ties density to current brush radius — dangerous with large smooth brushes); detail_refine_method options: 'SUBDIVIDE_COLLAPSE'(default — adds AND removes tris, prevents memory balloon), 'SUBDIVIDE'(only adds — can exhaust memory on long sessions), 'COLLAPSE'(only removes — good for cleanup pass); constant_detail_resolution: pixels-per-unit, 12px=fast coarse passes, 6–8px=fine detail; CONSTANT_DETAIL vs RELATIVE: RELATIVE uses bounding_box_diagonal as reference unit → large meshes get coarse micro-detail, CONSTANT gives same screen density everywhere; Symmetry: ts.sculpt.use_symmetry_x=True — must be set BEFORE first stroke or existing geometry not reflected; Brushes: bpy.ops.sculpt.brush_stroke() requires active viewport context + mouse positions (not scriptable from bpy alone — interactive only); Voxel Remesh: obj.data.remesh_voxel_size=0.025 (metres), obj.data.remesh_voxel_adaptivity=0.0 (uniform; >0 coarser in flat areas, can create T-poles), obj.data.use_remesh_smooth_normals=True; bpy.ops.object.voxel_remesh() — valid from Object Mode, no context override needed; algorithm: signed-distance-field at voxel_size → Marching Cubes → Quadriflow quad-dominant output; Quadriflow alignment: output quads tend to follow curvature flow, not grid-aligned — loop cuts roughly follow anatomical feature lines; voxel_size:mesh_size ratio 1:20 recommended (0.025m on 0.5m sphere→12k quads); Displace modifier approximation: mod.texture.noise_scale=2.8, turbulence=4.0 (extra octaves), mod.strength=0.18, mid_level=0.5, direction='NORMAL'; Multires modifier after remesh for normal-map bake pipeline: add MultresModifier, sculpt at level 3–4, bake(Cycles) NORMAL type to low-poly UV — see blender-tutorial-texture-baking-normal-ao; EEVEE ambient occlusion enhances surface read: scene.eevee.use_gtao=True, gtao_distance=0.15; GLB: Draco 6, +Y up, apply modifiers, export_apply=True; mutual exclusion: Dyntopo + Multires modifier cannot coexist — remove Multires before enabling Dyntopo; outside sources: Blender Manual Dyntopo CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/sculpt_paint/sculpting/tool_settings/dyntopo.html; Blender Manual Voxel Remesh CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/meshes/tools/voxel_remesh.html; glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 Khronos Group https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO |
| 2026-06-11 | blender-tutorial-armature-spline-ik-tentacle | Armature Spline IK — Flexible Tentacle / Tendril Rig (Blender 5.1) | blends/rigging/armature-spline-ik-tentacle/ | SPLINE_IK constraint placed on TIP bone — chain_count=BONE_COUNT reaches upward toward root (placing on root constrains only 1 bone); target=Bezier Curve object (NOT Armature); Blender samples curve at equal arc-length intervals per bone, positions and tangent direction per bone = curve derivative at sample point; xz_scale_mode options: NONE=rigid diameter, VOLUME_PRESERVE=rubber-hose (cross-section narrows as chain stretches to conserve volume), INVERSE_PRESERVE=inverse rubber, BONE_ORIGINAL=artist scale; y_scale_mode: FIT_CURVE=stretches each bone Y so total chain arc length matches Bezier arc length — almost always correct for tentacles; BONE_ORIGINAL=anatomical fixed-length segments (spine rigs); animation via Bezier handle keyframes on curve.data.splines[0].bezier_points[i].co + handle_left + handle_right — NOT on armature object; THREE KEYFRAME PATHS per point: co, handle_left, handle_right; bpy.ops.anim.keyframe_insert_by_name does NOT work on curve point properties — use keyframe_insert(data_path='co'); GLB EXPORT CRITICAL: glTF 2.0 has no Spline IK equivalent — exporter writes BIND POSE (straight chain) unless constraints are baked; depsgraph snapshot approach: scene.frame_set(N), dg=evaluated_depsgraph_get(), obj.evaluated_get(dg), meshes.new_from_object(eval_obj,depsgraph=dg) gives deformed static mesh for single-frame GLB; animated GLB requires bpy.ops.nla.bake(visual_keying=True, clear_constraints=True, bake_types={'POSE'}) first; chain construction: bones in +Y direction, b.use_connect=True welds head to parent tail; WHY tip bone not root: Blender walks chain_count steps toward root from constraint owner — root would only constrain itself; SKIN_RINGS >= 2×BONE_COUNT prevents visible pinching at joint boundaries; Armature deformer with manual vertex groups more reliable than auto-weights in headless bpy; Twist Mode=MINIMUM on curve data block suppresses torsion artefacts; holoflow_macros/armature_spline_ik_tentacle.py reusable build_spline_ik_rig() factory; outside sources: Blender Manual Spline IK Constraint CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/animation/constraints/tracking/spline_ik.html; blender-scripting MIT Nicolas Janakiev https://github.com/njanakiev/blender-scripting; glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 Khronos Group https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO |

| 2026-06-11 | blender-tutorial-shader-principled-volume-fog-column | Shader — Principled Volume: Procedural Fog Column (Blender 5.1) | blends/shading/shader-principled-volume-fog-column/ | Principled Volume shader maps to radiative transfer equation: Density=σ_s (scattering coefficient m⁻¹, NOT opacity slider); at σ_s=0.15 mean free path≈6.7m, translucent at close range, opaque at ~10m; Anisotropy=Henyey-Greenstein g parameter: g=0→isotropic scatter, g=0.5→50% more fwd scatter→god-ray beams when light behind fog; g=-0.5→back-scatter→halos around back-lit particles; Scatter Color=per-channel albedo of scatter events; Absorption Color=per-channel absorption albedo (white=no absorption=pure fog scatter); Emission Strength=0 for passive fog, raise for bioluminescent or fire-haze variant; WHY Object texture coordinates NOT Generated: Generated coords normalise to mesh bounding box→rescaling domain silently rescales noise density pattern; Object coords fixed in world space at 1 unit=1m so σ_s remains physically meaningful; ColorRamp low-cut at position 0.30: Noise Fac Gaussian distribution centred 0.5→without cut faint density everywhere→air-gap effect lost; cut at 0.30 forces bottom 30% to zero density→visible air gaps between wisps; Noise Detail=6.0 octaves, Distortion=0.30 domain warp breaks hexagonal Perlin bias (without distortion diagonal banding visible); EEVEE Next tile-based volumetric integrator: volumetric_tile_size='2'(2×2px highest quality, 4× cost vs default '8'), volumetric_samples=128 ray-march steps per tile per light; Spot Light ONLY for god rays (Area Light does NOT cast volumetric shadows in EEVEE Next 5.1); Spot Light use_shadow=True MANDATORY (most common omission→volume invisible); Shadow Soft Size 0.04m→near-point source→tight volumetric beams; shadow catcher floor: obj.is_shadow_catcher=True→receives volumetric shadows without its own material; GLB: no volume domain extension in mainline glTF 2.0; KHR_materials_volume (Apache-2.0 Khronos) covers surface transmission thickness NOT voxel domains; holoflow:volume_proxy custom property on domain cube→Three.js detects and replaces with custom ShaderMaterial; holoflow_macros/principled_volume_fog.py reusable apply_fog() helper; outside sources: Blender Manual Principled Volume CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/shader_nodes/shader/volume_principled.html; KHR_materials_volume Apache-2.0 Khronos Group https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/blob/main/extensions/2.0/Khronos/KHR_materials_volume/README.md |
| 2026-06-11 | blender-tutorial-gn-distribute-points-ground-cover-scatter | GN Distribute Points on Faces + Instance on Points — Procedural Ground Cover Scatter (Blender 5.1) | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-distribute-points-ground-cover-scatter/ | GeometryNodeDistributePointsOnFaces: distribution_type='POISSON_DISK' uses Mitchell's best-candidate algorithm — enforces distance_min between all pairs via rejection sampling; 'RANDOM' is pure Halton low-discrepancy sequence, O(n), but allows arbitrarily close neighbours; POISSON_DISK cost O(n·log n) negligible at scene density; inputs[0]=Mesh(GEOMETRY), inputs[1]=Selection(BOOL field), inputs[2]=Density(FLOAT field — points/m²), inputs[3]='Distance Min'(FLOAT, metres), inputs[4]=Seed(INT); outputs: Points(POINTCLOUD), Normal(VECTOR field), Rotation(EULER field); Named Attribute node: data_type='FLOAT', name='scatter_weight' reads vertex group as float field [0,1] per vertex, sampled across face by barycentric interpolation — face density = corner-weight average × Density Max; WHY vertex-group NOT texture: vertex groups update live in Weight Paint, no UV or bake step needed, consistent interpolation with mesh subdiv level; GeometryNodeInstanceOnPoints: inputs[0]=Points, inputs[1]=Selection, inputs[2]=Instance(GEOMETRY), inputs[3]='Pick Instance'(BOOL — TRUE=per-point random asset from collection), inputs[4]='Instance Index'(INT field — selects one child when Pick Instance=True), inputs[5]=Rotation(EULER), inputs[6]=Scale(VECTOR); GeometryNodeCollectionInfo: transform_space='RELATIVE', inputs[0]=Collection, inputs[1]='Separate Children'(BOOL, True=each child exposed as separate entry), inputs[2]='Reset Children'(BOOL); outputs[0]=Geometry; with Separate Children=True outputs a multi-geometry where each sub-geometry=one collection child; FunctionNodeAlignEulerToVector: axis='Z', pivot_axis='AUTO', inputs[0]=Rotation(EULER), inputs[1]=Factor(FLOAT 0..1), inputs[2]=Vector(VECTOR=face Normal from Distribute); outputs Rotation(EULER) that rotates +Z axis toward Normal — instance stands on surface regardless of face slope; Random Yaw: FunctionNodeRandomValue data_type='FLOAT_VECTOR', Min=(0,0,0), Max=(0,0,6.2832) — only Z component varies so yaw randomised, pitch/roll preserved from Align; GeometryNodeScaleInstances: inputs[3]=Scale(VECTOR) accepts float scalar when wired from scalar Random Value — broadcasts to (f,f,f); GeometryNodeRealizeInstances: REQUIRED before GLB export — without it exporter may write EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing instead of flat vertex buffer; Three.js/WebXR runtimes may not honour that extension; export_apply=True bakes NODES modifier; instance origin placement: scatter instances must have origin at bottom-most vertex (base of grass blade, base of pebble) or instances appear half-sunk; Collection exclude from view layer: layer_collection.children[col.name].exclude=True hides scatter pieces from direct rendering while GN still references them; GLB: export_apply=True, Draco level 6, WebP textures; outside sources: Blender Manual Distribute Points on Faces CC0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/point/distribute_points_on_faces.html; Poly Haven CC0 Jarrod Hasler Rico Cilliers https://polyhaven.com (CC0 plant/rock assets for use as scatter instances); AmbientCG CC0 https://ambientcg.com (sibling CC0 PBR material library) |

| 2026-06-11 | blender-tutorial-eevee-next-irradiance-sphere-probe | EEVEE Next — Irradiance Volume + Sphere Probe: Baked Indirect Lighting (Blender 5.1) | blends/lighting/eevee-next-irradiance-sphere-probe/ | bpy.ops.object.lightprobe_add(type='GRID'): Irradiance Volume probe — stores L2 spherical harmonics per voxel; each voxel captures irradiance from 6 cube faces; diffuse surfaces interpolate between nearest grid points for position-dependent GI; bpy.ops.object.lightprobe_add(type='SPHERE'): Sphere Probe — bakes one HDR cubemap from probe centre; pre-filtered into PMREM mip chain (gi_cubemap_resolution='256' → 256×256px per face × 6 faces); glossy/metallic surfaces within influence_distance sample this cubemap; BAKE REQUIREMENT: both probe types inert until baked via Properties›Render›Indirect Lighting›Bake Indirect Lighting; bake requires OpenGL context (EEVEE rasterises each cubemap face on GPU) — CANNOT trigger headlessly from bpy script alone; bake cache stored inside .blend file (~20 MB+ per probe at 256px); NOT exported to GLB (glTF 2.0 has no irradiance-volume extension — probe data silently discarded by glTF-Blender-IO); probe.data.influence_distance: radius of probe effect sphere (Sphere Probe only); probe.data.falloff: [0,1] fraction of influence_distance used for smooth blending at boundary; probe.data.clip_start: near clip for cubemap capture — must be >0 to avoid self-intersection artefacts (dark circular patches on chrome at clip_start=0.0); probe.data.clip_end: far clip; probe.data.grid_resolution_x/y/z: voxel counts per axis (Irradiance Volume only); probe.data.custom_parallax: Bool — when True allows separate parallax_distance for interior corner reflection correction; Sphere Probe blending: multiple probes weight by inverse distance from surface; EEVEE composites at render time; gi_cubemap_resolution='256': per-face resolution for all Sphere Probes; gi_irradiance_pool_size='32': atlas tile count (max 1024 voxels at '32'); scene.eevee.use_gtao + use_ssr complement probe data for screen-space effects; WebXR cubemap extraction: render 360° equirectangular from probe location (Camera type=Panoramic, Panorama Type=Equirectangular) → Three.js PMREMGenerator.fromEquirectangular() → mesh.material.envMap; Visibility Collection on probe: prevents object from being captured in its own probe cubemap (avoids bright blob artefact); outside sources: Blender Manual Light Probes Introduction CC-BY-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/eevee/light_probes/introduction.html; Blender Manual Irradiance Volumes CC-BY-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/eevee/light_probes/irradiance_volumes.html; three-stdlib PMREMGenerator MIT Poimandres/Mrdoob et al. https://github.com/pmndrs/three-stdlib |

| 2026-06-11 | blender-tutorial-cycles-light-path-glass-fireflies | Cycles — Light Path Node: Glass Without Fireflies (Blender 5.1) | blends/rendering/cycles-light-path-glass-fireflies/ | ShaderNodeLightPath outputs: Is Camera Ray / Is Shadow Ray / Is Diffuse Ray / Is Reflection Ray / Is Transmission Ray / Is Volume Scatter Ray / Is Singular Ray / Ray Depth / Diffuse Depth / Glossy Depth / Transparent Depth / Transmission Depth / Volume Depth / Total Depth; Is Shadow Ray mix trick: ShaderNodeMixShader with Factor=Is Shadow Ray, Shader[1]=Principled BSDF (Transmission=1.0, IOR=1.517), Shader[2]=Transparent BSDF — shadow rays see Transparent (no caustic variance), camera/reflection/transmission rays see glass (correct refraction); WHY TRICK WORKS: caustic fireflies arise from paths that multiply BSDF values along Transmission → Reflection → Transmission chain — extreme variance on rare focal-point paths; shadow rays evaluate occlusion only (not visible colour), so routing them through Transparent removes high-variance paths without affecting refracted image; Transparent Depth vs Transmission Depth: Transparent counts alpha-shadow/Transparent BSDF bounces; Transmission counts refractive glass crossings — keep transparent_max_bounces ≥ 2× nesting depth when using Is Shadow Ray trick (shadow rays now consume transparent budget); scene.cycles.clamp_direct=8.0 + clamp_indirect=4.0: caps per-sample radiance contribution — violates energy conservation but losses localised to statistically-uncovergeable paths; scene.cycles.caustics_refractive=False + caustics_reflective=False: renderer-level caustic toggle, consistent with shader trick (redundant if trick active, costs nothing to disable both); per-object ray visibility: obj.visible_shadow=False = nuclear option (zero shadow); Is Shadow Ray trick = surgical (soft transparent shadow preserved); object.is_shadow_catcher=True: Cycles composites shadow onto transparent background, floor plane receives prism shadows without needing shadow-pass compositor setup; GLB: Principled BSDF Transmission=1.0 + IOR → KHR_materials_transmission extension; Light Path node Cycles-only, stripped by exporter; Three.js r155+ MeshPhysicalMaterial.transmission reads transmissionFactor from extension; holoflow_macros/cycles_light_path_glass.py build_glass_material() + configure_cycles_for_glass() helpers; outside sources: Blender Manual Light Path Node CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/shader_nodes/input/light_path.html; KHR_materials_transmission Apache-2.0 Khronos Group https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/tree/main/extensions/2.0/Khronos/KHR_materials_transmission; glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 Khronos Group https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO |

| 2026-06-12 | blender-tutorial-eevee-light-linking-character-rig | EEVEE-Next Light Linking + Shadow Linking — Three-Light Character Hero Rig (Blender 5.1) | blends/lighting/eevee-light-linking-character-rig/ | Light Linking (Blender 4.0+): per-light Receiver Collection — only objects inside it receive illumination from that light; Shadow Linking: per-light Blocker Collection — only objects inside it cast shadows for that light; both properties on the light OBJECT (not bpy.data.lights data block): light_obj.light_linking.receiver_collection=Collection, light_obj.shadow_linking.blocker_collection=Collection; when receiver_collection=None → light illuminates all (default); collection MUST be linked to scene.collection hierarchy before assignment (scene.collection.children.link(col)) — free-floating collection assigned silently but has no effect; EEVEE-Next evaluates against active View Layer — object in excluded View Layer collection = omitted from light-link evaluation even if geometrically present; three-light hero rig: KEY(warm area 130W) → receiver=char+floor, blocker=char only; FILL(cool area 45W) → unlinked (all objects); RIM(white spot 380W) → receiver=char only, shadow disabled (rim.data.use_shadow=False); design rationale: rim constrained to character prevents cyclorama hot-oval; key blocker limited to character prevents irrelevant floor-to-floor occlusion; fill unlinked so soft ambient wrap reaches full scene; render-time only: glTF 2.0 has no receiver-collection equivalent — silently discarded by glTF-Blender-IO; Three.js equivalent: Object3D.layers bitmask — rim_light.layers.set(0); rim_light.layers.enable(1); characterMesh.layers.enable(1) — only meshes sharing a layer bit receive illumination; Python collection operations: col.objects.link(obj), col.objects.unlink(obj), viewport updates immediately in Rendered mode; Outliner View Layer mode: collection checkbox vs X distinguishes included/excluded from render+light-link evaluation; outside sources: Blender Manual Light Object CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/lights/light_object.html; Blender 4.0 Release Notes Rendering CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://wiki.blender.org/wiki/Reference/Release_Notes/4.0/Rendering; three.js MIT mrdoob et al. https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js |

| 2026-06-12 | blender-tutorial-compositor-glare-filmgrain-tonemapping | Compositor — Glare Node + Film Grain + Tone Map: Cinematic Post-Processing Pipeline (Blender 5.1) | blends/compositing/compositor-glare-filmgrain-tonemapping/ | CompositorNodeGlare: glare_type options: FOG_GLOW=omnidirectional specular bloom (correct for curved reflective surfaces), STREAKS=anamorphic lens streak (correct for bokeh highlights/anamorphic cinema), SIMPLE_STAR=cross-hatch (night lights), GHOSTS=lens flare (backlit sun); threshold is scene-linear HDR value floor — pixels above it bloom; size=power-of-two radius in pixels (size=8→256px), max=9; mix 0=original only, 1=glow only; GLARE MUST PRECEDE TONE MAPPING: Glare reads HDR linear values; post-tone-map values clamped to [0,1] remove the peaks Glare needs; CompositorNodeTonemap: tonemap_type='PHOTORECEPTOR' (key=target middle-grey analogous to 18% grey-card exposure, offset=black lift 1.0=none, gamma=output gamma 1.0=linear); DOUBLE-TONEMAPPING TRAP: Blender 5.1 AgX view transform applies its own tone curve through Color Management display engine; adding a ToneMap compositor node while AgX is active double-curves → crushed blacks + muddy highlights; two safe pipelines: A=AgX view transform + NO ToneMap node (production default), B=Raw view transform + ToneMap node (demonstration/manual control); Film Grain pattern: generate noise image via Python (bpy.data.images.new + pixel array), R=G=B monochrome (per-channel noise = sensor defect appearance not film), values centred 0.5±deviation; Screen blend (1-(1-a)(1-b)) avoids highlight clipping vs Add blend; GRAIN MUST FOLLOW TONE MAP: scene-linear noise is luminance-weighted (10cd/m² pixel gets 10× grain of 1cd/m² pixel → shadow explosion); display-referred (post-tone-map) space = perceptually uniform grain matching silver-halide film chemistry; CompositorNodeLensdist: Distort negative=barrel (outward bow), positive=pincushion; Dispersion=lateral chromatic aberration (red/blue shift outward from centre); CompositorNodeEllipseMask: width/height [0,1] as fraction of frame, x/y=centre [0,1]; Gaussian Blur for vignette falloff; remap [0,1]→[1-depth,1] via Multiply+Add Math chain so mask centre=1.0 (unchanged) and corners=1-depth; VIGNETTE MUST BE MULTIPLY NOT ADD-BLACK: adding black subtracts channels equally → on saturated edges one channel hits zero first → hue shift; Multiply scales all channels by same scalar → hue preserved (same principle as Lightroom Post-Crop Vignette Highlight Priority); make_grain_image() creates deterministic noise (seed=42) at render resolution — deterministic seed ensures grain is consistent across re-renders and versions; outside sources: filmic-blender MIT Troy Sobotka https://github.com/sobotka/filmic-blender; Blender Compositor Manual CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/compositing/; OpenColorIO Apache-2.0 Academy Software Foundation https://github.com/AcademySoftwareFoundation/OpenColorIO |

| 2026-06-12 | blender-tutorial-shader-holographic-panel-emission-fresnel | Shader — Holographic Interface Panel: Emission + Fresnel + Voronoi Hex Grid + Animated Scan Lines (Blender 5.1) | blends/shading/shader-holographic-panel-emission-fresnel/ | Voronoi DISTANCE_TO_EDGE: 0 at cell boundaries → invert → CONSTANT ColorRamp threshold = hex grid lines; HEX_LINE_DUTY=0.13; Randomness=0→honeycomb, 1→blobs; Wave Texture BANDS/Y: varies with UV V = horizontal stripes; CONSTANT ramp SCAN_LINE_DUTY=0.15 = bright at wave peak; Mapping Y Location driver frame*0.0045 = scan lines scroll; Fresnel IOR=1.42: 0 face-on, 1 at silhouette; MULTIPLY_ADD fresnel×2.2+1.0 = rim amplifier 1.0–3.2; combined_mask×amplifier×4.5→Emission Strength; Alpha=BASE(0.05)+pattern×0.55+fresnel×0.40 clamped; surface_render_method=FORWARD (EEVEE-Next sorted alpha, replaces blend_method); shadow_method=NONE; KHR_materials_emissive_strength auto-applied Emission>1.0→Three.js emissiveIntensity; alphaMode:BLEND in GLB JSON; outside sources: KhronosGroup/glTF KHR_materials_emissive_strength Apache-2.0 https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/tree/main/extensions/2.0/Khronos/KHR_materials_emissive_strength; KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 Julien Duroure https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO |

left-stop at 0.0 = full ±displacement → foam rubber look; scene.cycles.dicing_rate (pixels per micropolygon edge): 1.0=fine, 8.0=coarse; camera-adaptive = more geometry when camera is close; scene.cycles.preview_dicing_rate: separate viewport budget (8.0 typical); scene.cycles.max_subdivisions=12: caps per-face micropolygon count as safety valve against memory exhaustion; Subdivision Surface modifier levels_viewport=1: provides rough visual approximation in EEVEE solid/material-preview modes; use_adaptive_subdivision only evaluated during Cycles render pass; Baking displaced to normal map: duplicate low-poly sphere (no subdivision modifier), new material with active Image Texture node (Non-Color, 2048×2048), bpy.ops.object.bake(type='NORMAL', use_selected_to_active=True, cage_extrusion=0.25) — cage_extrusion must exceed DISPLACEMENT_SCALE (0.12 m here → cage=0.25); LINEAR gives unphysical gradients; gap VEIN_STOP_LO=0.35 to VEIN_STOP_HI=0.42 = 7% of wave period ≈ 1.4 cm per 0.2 m period at VEIN_FREQ=5; VectorMath MULTIPLY Z-compression (VEIN_Z_COMPRESS=0.45): non-uniform scale compresses Z-axis before Wave node — stretches bands into horizontal sweeping veins rather than tight ellipses; Color1=VEIN_COLOR, Color2=cloud tone; Principled BSDF v2 Subsurface Weight=0.12, Subsurface Radius=(0.05,0.04,0.03) m — R>G>B gives amber warm edge glow; RANDOM_WALK SSS (default 5.1): handles thin section correctly unlike BURLEY which overscatters at mesh boundaries; Coat Weight=1.0 + Coat Roughness=0.04: two-layer specular — tight coat above broader base specular; IOR=1.486 (calcite ordinary ray); Coat IOR inherited from base IOR — sets Brewster angle where coat reflectance peaks; Fresnel at slab edges: normal incidence → R=(n-1)²/(n+1)² ≈ 3.5%; grazing → R→1; explains edge gloss independently of SSS; GLB export writes KHR_materials_clearcoat (Coat Weight + Roughness); SSS baked to texture at export — no real-time SSS in Three.js MeshPhysicalMaterial at browser framerates; inputs: Target Geometry (GEOMETRY), Attribute (field), Source Position (VECTOR field — defaults to InputPosition when unconnected), Ray Direction (VECTOR field), Ray Length (FLOAT field); outputs: Is Hit (BOOL field), Hit Position (VECTOR field), Hit Normal (VECTOR field), Hit Distance (FLOAT field), Attribute (passthrough field); mapping property: INTERPOLATED (default) bilinearly interpolates attributes across hit triangle, NEAREST snaps to closest vertex; CRITICAL coordinate-space rule: source positions are in modifier object's LOCAL space; target geometry arrives via GeometryNodeObjectInfo with transform_space='RELATIVE' which pre-multiplies target world_matrix × modifier_object.matrix_world.inverted() — both in same space regardless of individual transforms; DO NOT use transform_space='ORIGINAL' unless both objects have identical world matrices; ray must be a unit vector but does not need to be normalized explicitly — non-unit direction still fires but Ray Length is not scaled; terrain apply-before-raycast rule: if terrain has pending GN modifier, dependency cycle risk — terrain GN evaluates same frame scatter GN requests it; bake terrain to plain mesh (bpy.ops.object.convert target='MESH') to break cycle; BooleanMath NOT on Is Hit → DeleteGeometry Selection removes boundary misses cleanly; FunctionNodeAlignEulerToVector axis='Z': maps instance local +Z to Hit Normal — flat quad lies on surface; FunctionNodeRandomValue data_type='FLOAT' with separate SCALE_SEED (≠ scatter seed) avoids correlation between point density and instance size; CombineXYZ scalar→vec3 for uniform InstanceOnPoints.Scale; export_apply=True in bpy.ops.export_scene.gltf bakes GN modifier to static mesh before writing — GLB has no live GN graph, compatible with all glTF 2.0 loaders; | 2026-06-13 | blender-tutorial-modifier-array-curve-deform-chain-rope | Array Modifier + Curve Deform: Procedural Chain / Rope Along Curve (Blender 5.1) | blends/modifiers/modifier-array-curve-deform-chain-rope/ | Array fit_type='FIT_CURVE': modifier queries guide curve arc-length each depsgraph evaluation and sets count=ceil(arc/segment_length) automatically — no manual integer entry; use_constant_offset + constant_offset_displace=(link_pitch,0,0): slides copies end-to-end along local X; use_relative_offset=False: MUST be disabled when using constant offset or copies double-shift; Curve modifier deform_axis='POS_X': maps local X onto curve tangent direction (Frenet–Serret frame); vertex projection: each vertex finds nearest curve t-parameter, tangent→new X, curve normal→Y, binormal→Z — preserves cross-section profile at every bend; CRITICAL modifier stack order: Array must be ABOVE Curve in stack — Array fires first producing tiled straight strip, Curve fires on that output bending it; if reversed, Curve bends single link, then Array copies the already-bent link at wrong angles; two-link unit cell: one horizontal half-torus + one 90°-rotated half-torus in same mesh object, total X-extent = 2×link_pitch; Array tiles the pair → alternating horizontal/vertical links (real chain interlocking topology) without any special logic; half-torus arc = π from -90° to +90° so open ends face ±X and adjacent link threads through the gap; minor cross-section radius must be < (outer_radius - inner_radius)/2 of adjacent link to avoid interpenetration; rope variant: Object Offset mode — Empty translated by seg_length along X + rotated 15° around X; Array accumulates transforms multiplicatively so copy N is rotated 15°×N; cumulative twist simulates three-strand braid rhythm; 15° chosen not 120° because 120° reads as smooth spiral not plaited braid at viewport framerate; guide curve origin MUST be at first control point — Curve modifier starts wrapping from object origin, not from curve start; misplaced origin shifts chain start mid-path; fix with bpy.ops.object.origin_set(type='ORIGIN_GEOMETRY'); GLB export: export_apply=True bakes Array+Curve to static mesh — glTF 2.0 has no procedural modifier concept; for runtime path control in WebXR: export guide points as vertex array, reconstruct with THREE.CatmullRomCurve3 + TubeGeometry; use_merge_vertices=False preserves chain link islands (required for per-link physics later); setting too high makes blurry rough surfaces trace many useless steps; too low loses satin-metal reflections; 0.45 keeps chrome + brushed metal while excluding matte surfaces; ssr_thickness: each screen pixel implicitly represents a solid slab of this depth; rays that intersect within the slab register a hit; too thin → false misses on thick objects (dark halo at silhouette edges); too thick → reflections leak through walls; ssr_border_fade: normalised fraction of screen width over which SSR fades at edges; 0=hard pop when objects pan into frame; 0.075–0.1 = visually invisible blend to probe value; ssr_firefly_fac clamps extreme bright samples that cause bright white spots in reflection (common on area lights seen at grazing); Sphere Probe is the PROBE fallback: probe.data.influence_distance must encompass entire scene — too small → surfaces fall back to unlit black rather than ambient probe; one probe at scene centre covers all surfaces; probe cubemap is static (baked once) unless bpy.ops.scene.light_cache_bake() is called; for moving reflective objects: per-object sphere probe overrides or accept SSR-only for dynamic surfaces; shade_smooth() mandatory before SSR: flat normals produce polygon-faceted pattern in SSR reflection buffer; IcoSphere subdivisions=4 → 642 verts = smooth enough for SSR without excess geometry; IOR input on Metallic=1.0 material has NO effect — governs dielectric lobe suppressed at metallic=1; scene.eevee.ray_tracing_method in Blender 5.1: 'PROBE'=probe-only, 'SCREEN'=SSR (use_ssr path), 'HARDWARE'=Vulkan RT cores (RTX/RDNA3+) — traces against actual geometry not depth buffer, handles off-screen; use_ssr properties remain and map to SCREEN; WebXR decision: keep metallic props below roughness 0.4 for SSR coverage, or bake per-object sphere probes for satin surfaces above cutoff; outside sources: Blender Manual EEVEE Screen Space Reflections CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/5.1/render/eevee/render_settings/screen_space_reflections.html; wrap_mode='ON_SURFACE' moves regardless of side (retopo); 'PROJECT' fires along axis and misses if ray doesn't hit target; 'NEAREST_VERTEX' snaps to target vertex not face — fast but coarse; CRITICAL stack order: Lattice (index 0, applied first) reshapes mesh → Shrinkwrap (index 1, applied second) snaps contact ring on already-shaped mesh; CYCLES (FModifierCycles): mode_before/mode_after=REPEAT/REPEAT_OFFSET/MIRROR; cycles_before/after=0 means infinite; remaps time axis so depsgraph evaluates curve at looped frame position; zero alloc vs NLA strip overhead; evaluation order critical: CYCLES at index 0, NOISE at index 1 — CYCLES remaps time THEN noise adds offset, ensuring consistent organic variation per cycle; reversed stack doubles noise at boundary and creates pop artifact; NOISE (FModifierNoise): blend_type=ADD adds random offset to base curve value; REPLACE discards base entirely (fully procedural jitter channel); MULTIPLY amplitude-modulates base; scale=time-frequency of noise pattern (higher=faster fluctuation); strength=amplitude; phase=random seed (desynchronise objects by varying phase); depth=octave count (1=smooth, 4=jagged); use_restricted_range+blend_in/blend_out=fade modifier in/out over frame range without re-keying base action; STEPPED (FModifierStepped): frame_step=quantisation period (4=holds 4 frames before jumping); snap_in_frame=which frame within block is sampled (0=first, step/2=mid-block avoids motion-blur artifact at boundary); cel-animation on-twos=frame_step=2; ratchet gear=frame_step=N with LINEAR base keyframes; CAMERA SHAKE RECIPE: seed two static keyframes on camera loc, add NOISE ADD blend_type to loc.X+loc.Y with different phase values (17 vs 91) — desynchronises axes so shake feels 2D not diagonal-correlated; EXPORT BOUNDARY: F-Curve modifiers not in glTF spec; bake via Object▸Animation▸Bake Action Visual Keying Step=1 Clear Modifiers before GLB export; Step=2 halves keyframe count for coarse channels; outside sources: F-Curve Modifiers Blender Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 Blender Docs Team https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/graph_editor/fcurves/modifiers.html; OIDN quality vs samples: Normal+Albedo guides raise effective quality by ~4-8× sample-count equivalent; WHY NORMAL MATTERS: without Normal OIDN blurs across geometric edges treating adjacent pixels on different surfaces as spatially correlated; WHY ALBEDO MATTERS: without DiffCol OIDN cannot separate illumination noise from colour saturation — result shows colour drift in mid-tones; HDR SPECULAR: disabling use_hdr clamps specular highlights to [0,1] before OIDN reconstructs as flat white patches — loss of specular shape and IOR-correct Fresnel gradient; TEMPORAL DENOISING: compositor Denoise node is per-frame spatial only → animated sequences flicker; use scene.cycles.use_denoising=True scene.cycles.denoiser='OPENIMAGEDENOISE' for temporal denoising of animations; CAUSTIC FIREFLIES: OIDN cannot reconstruct single-sample caustic events — suppress via sample_clamp_indirect or enable Manifold Path Sampling; HAIR EDGES: SSS boundaries and fine strands over-smoothed below 8spp; blend band [0.20,0.80] identifies joint-region verts; parabolic blend_strength=(1-|forearm_fraction-0.5|×2) concentrates correction at 50/50 apex; Gaussian falloff along Y axis (sigma=15% of bone length) localises correction; correction direction: +Z at rest for posterior elbow when arm rests along +Y; shape key data[vi].co is ABSOLUTE position not delta (delta computed at draw time relative to Basis); key_block.driver_add("value") adds driver on shape key value FCurve; driver var type=TRANSFORMS: mode-agnostic unlike SINGLE_PROP on rotation_euler (silently returns 0 if bone switches to QUATERNION); transform_type=ROT_X transform_space=LOCAL_SPACE; LOCAL_SPACE measures forearm-vs-parent angle → fires on elbow flex NOT on whole-arm world rotation; expression=max(0.0,min(1.0,-var/1.5708)); negate because forearm flexion is negative X rotation; 1.5708=π/2=90°; replace with radian angle of worst collapse for specific rig; rotation_mode pitfall: SINGLE_PROP on rotation_euler[0] returns 0 silently when bone switched to QUATERNION → always use TRANSFORMS for production rigs; elbow_corrective value=0.0 at rest; driver activates during pose; GLB export: drivers not in glTF spec; bake via Object▸Animation▸Bake Action Visual Keying=True to export morph-target weight animation; Blender 5.1 arm_obj.shape_key_add(name=…,from_mix=False) always starts from Basis; from_mix=True captures current deformed state (wrong for corrective use); fcurve.driver.is_valid=True forces immediate evaluation after driver creation; SampleIndex data_type='FLOAT_VECTOR' reads vec3 attributes at arbitrary index — requires explicit Geometry input connected to sim_in.outputs['Geometry'] and the evaluated geometry at that frame; GeometryNodeAttributeStatistic data_type='FLOAT_VECTOR' Mean output = global flock centroid (VECTOR); COHESION: SUBTRACT(centroid-self_pos) → SCALE(COH_WEIGHT=0.005); weak intentionally — acts on global mean vs separation/alignment which act on single neighbour; SPEED CLAMP: NORMALIZE→SCALE with Math(MIN,MAX_SPEED) — avoids divide-by-zero unlike divide-by-length pattern; Euler integration: VectorMath(ADD, pos+vel) → SetPosition; order matters — SetPosition before StoreNamedAttribute; StoreNamedAttribute vel → SimulationOutput closes feedback loop; FunctionNodeAlignEulerToVector axis='Z' pivot_axis='AUTO' — cone +Z faces travel direction; second InputNamedAttribute('vel') OUTSIDE zone reads post-update velocity; InstanceOnPoints Rotation input takes AlignEulerToVector output directly — no separate RotateInstances node; Marsaglia method for uniform sphere velocity seeding — Gaussian triplet normalised; rejection sampling for uniform sphere position (acceptance 52%); sequential frame_set(1..N) + view_layer.update() mandatory to build simulation cache headlessly; cone parked at Y=-9999 as GN instance template — seed position irrelevant | 5.1 | CC0 |

marching cubes mesh is always manifold (water-tight) regardless of input mesh quality — makes this a robust clean-up pipeline for boolean results on messy meshes; GeometryNodeRealizeInstances MUST precede MeshToVolume — GN instances carry no triangle geometry until realised, MeshToVolume on unresolved instances produces empty volume; Adaptivity=0 produces dense uniform triangulation suitable for Draco GLB and 3D-print; Adaptivity=0.3 acceptable for viewport preview; POISSON_DISK scatter on emitter prevents sphere pile-ups that create inverted-normal cavities; Rule of thumb: Exterior_Band = Threshold + 0.05; Voxel_count = (bbox/voxel_size)³ but narrow-band only allocates shell → 0.035m voxels for 2m blob ≈ 60k voxels not 374k; blender --background --python blueprint.py outputs blob_fusion.blend + blob_fusion.glb; collapses sorted by cost (merged vertex at quadric minimum), executed greedily until Ratio target reached; use_symmetry=True constrains collapse pairs to bilaterally symmetric half-edges — correct for X-Mirror character meshes, counterproductive for asymmetric scans; DISSOLVE: Planar mode dissolves adjacent face pairs whose normal angular difference < angle_limit (radians); produces ZERO visual change on flat architecture / hard-surface because coplanar faces carry no curvature information; use_dissolve_boundaries=False preserves UV-seam edges and sharp-flagged edges even if geometrically coplanar — setting True destroys UV islands silently; after dissolve n-gons must be triangulated by exporter — same visual result but preview face count in Blender drops dramatically; UNSUBDIV: reverses Catmull-Clark subdivision by merging 2×2 quad blocks into single source quads; requires SubDiv applied BEFORE adding Decimate — if SubDiv is still a live modifier above Decimate in stack, UNSUBDIV sees pre-subdivision topology and cannot find the 2×2 blocks; max safe iterations = number of SubDiv levels applied; exceeding limit causes pole-cap artefacts on icospheres (irregular patch inheritance); all modes use export_apply=True at GLB export to materialise modifier without touching source .blend; Decimate+Draco are complementary: Decimate reduces triangle COUNT, Draco reduces bytes per triangle; 50k-tri displaced sphere at COLLAPSE ratio=0.12 + Draco L6 → ~6k triangles ~15KB; Planar dissolve on 6500-tri over-subdivided cube → 12 tris ~2KB; mod.angle_limit in RADIANS not degrees (math.radians() required); bmesh.ops.subdivide_edges(bm, edges=bm.edges[:], cuts=8, use_grid_fill=True) creates dense interior coplanar topology for Planar demo; | 2026-06-20 | blender-tutorial-gn-duplicate-elements-edge-fins-spines | GN Duplicate Elements — Edge Domain Fins: Geometric Spike Shell (Blender 5.1) | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-duplicate-elements-edge-fins-spines/ | GeometryNodeDuplicateElements domain='EDGE' clones every edge of a mesh as a disconnected 2-vertex segment preserving all named attributes on those elements; key distinction from Instance on Points: instances require a point cloud and sit AT discrete points, Duplicate Elements clones topology elements themselves — edges stay edges and span two vertices with correct edge-length-awareness; key distinction from Extrude Mesh on original: extrude on original fuses fin quads to the body sharing vertices at the hinge, making independent material assignment impossible without post-selection; Duplicate Elements creates completely disconnected copies forming their own mesh island from creation; Normal node evaluates on face domain; duplicated edge geometry has no faces — Normal returns zero on face-free mesh; solution: run GeometryNodeFieldOnDomain(Normal, data_type=FLOAT_VECTOR, domain=EDGE) on the ORIGINAL mesh BEFORE duplication to average adjacent face normals onto each edge; then GeometryNodeStoreNamedAttribute(data_type=FLOAT_VECTOR, domain=EDGE, Name="fin_dir") stamps that averaged direction per edge; DuplicateElements preserves named attributes verbatim on copied elements so "fin_dir" survives intact; DuplicateElements.Amount=1: one copy per edge; Amount>N: N copies per edge stacked at same position (useful for feathered multi-fin look with Duplicate Index offset for per-copy variation); DuplicateElements.Duplicate Index output: integer field mapping each copy to its source element index — use with Math MODULO for per-copy seeding of noise or random; after duplication GeometryNodeInputNamedAttribute(data_type=FLOAT_VECTOR, Name="fin_dir") reads stored direction; noise-varied height: ShaderNodeTexNoise(3D) → Fac 0..1 → ShaderNodeMath MULTIPLY_ADD(Fac, range, min) → remaps to NOISE_MIN..NOISE_MAX → Math MULTIPLY with Fin Height → ShaderNodeVectorMath SCALE(fin_dir, varied_height) → per-edge offset vector; GeometryNodeExtrudeMesh mode='EDGES' sweeps each disconnected edge into a quad fin; Individual parameter irrelevant when edges are already disconnected (no shared vertices to merge); GeometryNodeSetMaterial assigns fin material to extruded quads only; GeometryNodeJoinGeometry combines original sphere body (from Group Input.Geometry directly) with fin quads — body and fins are separate mesh islands in one GLB mesh; export_apply=True required; GLB Draco 70-80% size reduction on this topology because fin quads form regular edge-proportional grid; FieldOnDomain input socket index: for data_type=FLOAT_VECTOR the Vector socket is inputs[2] (not inputs[0]); socket order for type-dependent GN nodes: Float=0, Int=1, Vector=2, Color=3, Bool=4, Rotation=5; VectorMath SCALE: inputs[0]=Vector, inputs[3]=Scale (NOT inputs[1]); StoreNamedAttribute socket order: inputs[0]=Geometry, inputs[1]=Selection, inputs[2]=Name, inputs[3]=Value; NamedAttribute.inputs['Name'].default_value string is case-sensitive and whitespace-sensitive — copy-paste recommended; bgl used raw OpenGL calls via ctypes; gpu module is a platform abstraction over Metal/Vulkan/OpenGL backends; gpu.shader.from_builtin('SMOOTH_COLOR') compiles a pre-baked GLSL shader accepting pos(vec3)+color(vec4) per vertex; attribute name 'SMOOTH_COLOR' was '3D_SMOOTH_COLOR' in Blender 3.x — renamed in 4.0 alongside removal of 3D_ prefix on all builtins; gpu_extras.batch.batch_for_shader(shader, 'LINES', {'pos':verts, 'color':colors}) constructs GPUBatch from Python lists — LINES primitive interprets consecutive pairs as individual segments; SpaceView3D.draw_handler_add(cb, (), 'WINDOW', 'POST_VIEW') binds callback to draw loop; POST_VIEW fires AFTER all geometry passes while depth buffer is live — depth_test_set('LESS_EQUAL') reads depth so arrows are clipped by occluding geometry; POST_PIXEL fires after depth resolve in screen-pixel coords — correct for 2D HUD only; draw_handler_add returns opaque handle — MUST be stored at module level for teardown; draw_handler_remove(_handle, 'WINDOW') cleans up; missing remove = dangling callback = EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION on next draw after Python module unload; guard pattern: if _handle is not None: remove first, then add new — prevents duplicate callbacks on hot-reload; normal transform: inverse-transpose of 3x3 world matrix (mat.inverted().transposed().to_3x3()); uniform-scale shortcut mat.to_3x3() is WRONG for non-uniform scale (squash/stretch bones, blendshape modifiers) — normals shear toward stretched axis; colour gradient: t=(w_normal.z+1)/2 maps [-1,+1] to [0,1]; lerp(COL_DOWN, COL_UP, t) gives blue(−Z)→red(+Z) arrow colour; gpu.state.depth_mask_set(False) — read depth for occlusion but do NOT write; writing depth from overlay corrupts scene depth buffer causing z-fighting on Blender's own gizmos; restore gpu.state.line_width_set(1.0) and depth_mask_set(True) after drawing — GPU state is shared across draw callbacks in same frame; _build_batch rebuilds on EVERY draw call = one GPUBatch per viewport refresh; acceptable for meshes <10k faces; for larger meshes cache the batch and invalidate via depsgraph_update_post handler triggered by MESH type change; area.tag_redraw() queues immediate viewport repaint after handler registration; EXACT is 3× slower per-Boolean but sub-second for sub-50k meshes; Hole Tolerant sub-mode (mod.use_hole_tolerant=True) performs flood-fill to determine interior/exterior for open-bottomed cutters that do not pierce base mesh — required for shallow recesses; Bevel above only chamfers original base-mesh edges before any cuts; mod.limit_method='ANGLE' + mod.angle_limit=math.radians(45) prevents Bevel chamfering low-angle polygon seams created by Exact on cylinder port rims; NON_MANIFOLD (=Complex, Python solidify_mode='NON_MANIFOLD', introduced Blender 2.82) uses halfedge-miter algorithm that inserts a diagonal strip at each concave vertex instead of extending offset faces — result is manifold at all dihedral angles; canonical test: L-shaped wall corner — two quads sharing corner edge at 90° — Simple inner corner self-intersects, Complex closes with clean 45° miter diagonal; solidify_mode='SIMPLE'|'NON_MANIFOLD'; thickness = float metres; offset = -1.0..+1.0 scalar; -1.0 = outer (source) face stationary, inner shell grows inward; 0.0 = centred; +1.0 = outer shell grows, inner stationary; nonmanifold_thickness_mode='FIXED'|'EVEN'|'CONSTRAINTS' — FIXED constant along face normal, EVEN projects onto bisecting planes (uniform visual thickness at joints, wider corner geometry), CONSTRAINTS best for sharp-angle hard-surface; nonmanifold_boundary_mode='NONE'|'FLAT'|'ROUND' — FLAT keeps boundary rim coplanar with source face edges, ROUND bevels into arc (organic/pipe surfaces); rim_fill_mode='CLOSED' required for watertight 3D print mesh; mod.use_rim=True, mod.use_rim_only=False; material slot system: material_offset=int shifts shell face material index relative to source face (both inner and outer shell get same shifted index — normals distinguish direction, not materials); material_offset_rim=int shifts rim face index — e.g. source_slot=0, material_offset=0, material_offset_rim=1 puts rim faces on slot 1 without Edit Mode selection; inner vs outer distinct material requires apply + manual face selection or GN attribute shader bridge; stack position: Solidify[0] above Boolean modifiers so cutters pierce full wall thickness; Solidify above Subdivision Surface so SubDiv sees the full solidified topology (reversed order = dense source plane subdivided before solidification, wasted vertices); Even Thickness (Simple mode mod.use_even_offset=True) preserves uniform thickness on curved surfaces; Complex mode equivalent: nonmanifold_thickness_mode='EVEN'; apply-on-duplicate export: duplicate → apply all modifiers → transform_apply → export_scene.gltf Draco L6 WebP Y-up → delete duplicate; | 2026-06-21 | blender-tutorial-modifier-wireframe-edge-tube-neon-skeleton | Modifier — Wireframe: Edge-Tube Neon Skeleton for WebXR and Low-Poly Display (Blender 5.1) | blends/modifiers/modifier-wireframe-edge-tube-neon-skeleton/ | Wireframe modifier converts every edge to a 4-sided tube (quad strip along edge, mitre-joined at each vertex); two sub-modes: use_even_offset=False (Simple) extends each quad strip along edge direction — at high-valence vertices strips overlap producing star-burst artefact (5-valent icosphere poles look like overlapping pentagons); use_even_offset=True (Even) computes bisecting plane between each pair of adjacent incident edges and clips both strips at that plane — clean mitre joint at any valence, cap polygon face count equals vertex valence; use_even_offset=True is the correct default for all practical use cases; use_replace=True removes all source faces leaving only the tube skeleton — correct for display objects, geodesic sculptures, neon wireframe; use_replace=False keeps source faces + tubes coexist in same mesh — enables material_offset system for emissive edge-cage overlay on solid meshes; material_offset=N shifts tube face material slot index by N relative to source face slot — slot 0 = opaque PBR base, slot 1 = emission tube; no face selection in Edit Mode required; use_boundary=True includes open boundary edges (edges adjacent to only one face) — essential for any open-shell mesh (flat plane, hemisphere, disk); closed meshes (all edges have 2 adjacent faces) unaffected by this flag; use_relative=False: thickness is absolute world units, same radius on every edge; use_relative=True: thickness is fraction of each edge's individual length — maintains visual proportion on irregular topology where edge lengths vary by 10x or more; modifier stack: Wireframe ABOVE Solidify to cage a solidified wall (below = Solidify receives tube mesh, gives wall thickness to tube quads = hollow double-walled tubes); Wireframe ABOVE Boolean to cage a post-cut solid (below = Boolean operates on tube quads, carving partial tubes); export: export_apply=True in export_scene.gltf() materialises modifier at export time; apply-on-duplicate pattern keeps source modifier live in .blend; Wireframe tube topology: 30 edges on ICO-level-1 sphere → 30 × 4 quads per tube + 12 × 5-face vertex caps = 180 quads + 60 cap tris; bmesh.ops.create_grid(x_segments=4, y_segments=4, size=1.2) creates 4×4 grid of 16 quads, 25 verts, 40 interior + 16 boundary edges; make_neon_mat: Emission node plugged directly into Output::Surface — no Principled BSDF — maximises luminance on dark background regardless of HDRI brightness; mod.particle_system references the auto-created PS; pset = mod.particle_system.settings is the configure-in-place data-block; pset.type='EMITTER': time-windowed burst (frame_start→frame_end), each particle lives lifetime frames then dies — distinct from 'HAIR' which is static distribution ignoring all force fields; pset.lifetime_random=0.35: stagger factor; particle dies between lifetime*(1-random) and lifetime — avoids synchronised disappearance at a single frame; pset.physics_type='NEWTON': velocity integrated each frame, force fields applied; pset.normal_factor=outward velocity along face normal; pset.factor_random=per-particle random velocity addend; pset.angular_velocity_mode='RAND'+angular_velocity_factor=2.5: independent random tumble per shard; pset.effector_weights.gravity=0.25: fraction of -9.81 applied — floaty shard motion; pset.render_type='OBJECT'+instance_object=shard: each particle rendered as clone of shard_instance at particle position/rotation/scale; pset.size_random=0.40: ±40% scale scatter; bpy.ops.object.effector_add(type='TURBULENCE') creates empty with field.type pre-assigned; field.strength=10.0 velocity kick magnitude; field.size=0.6 m noise cell size — smaller=finer turbulence, one cell across burst radius gives each shard distinct arc; field.noise=0.5 secondary perturbation on primary field (0=laminar vortex, 1=fully stochastic); field.use_global_coords=True: world-space noise, rotation-invariant; depsgraph readback: particles do NOT exist in emitter.data; alive_state cycles UNBORN→ALIVE→DYING→DEAD; p.rotation is mathutils.Quaternion WXYZ order; assign via cpy.rotation_mode='QUATERNION'+cpy.rotation_quaternion=p.rotation NOT p.rotation.to_euler() — Euler conversion silently mangles fast-tumbling shards near gimbal lock (>180°/frame rotation at poles); Simple (not Catmull-Clark) subdivision: Catmull-Clark repositions base verts toward limit surface (shrinks mesh ~3%), so Cast target at factor=1.0 targets original BLOB_RADIUS but mesh is already smaller — gap visible; Simple splits edges at midpoint without repositioning — Cast target matches original geometry exactly; CastCuboid.use_z=False: Z axis left to object.scale.z keyframes for explicit volume compensation: squash scale (1.3,1.3,0.4) ≈ original volume (4/3)πr³; ratio STEP_SIZE/AGG_RADIUS ≈ 0.85 gives natural branch-dominant fractal; ratio > 2.0 = tunnelling (walkers jump past crystal); ratio < 0.25 = dense blob; JoinGeometry pairs: Crystal=old+stamped-aggregators sim_out.Geometry; | 2026-06-27 | blender-tutorial-gn-accumulate-field-weighted-stripe-tube | GN Accumulate Field — Proportional UV Stripes: Noise-Weighted Arc-Length Tiling on a Curve Tube (Blender 5.1) | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-accumulate-field-weighted-stripe-tube/ | GeometryNodeAccumulateField (bl_idname 'GeometryNodeAccumulateField'): prefix-sum field node operating on any domain (POINT/EDGE/FACE/CORNER); data_type FLOAT/INT/FLOAT_VECTOR; inputs: inputs['Value'] (field to sum), inputs['Group ID'] (INT, per-group restart), inputs['Sort Index'] (INT, ordering within group, defaults to element index 0..N-1); outputs: outputs['Leading'] (exclusive prefix sum — total of all PRECEDING elements; 0 for element 0), outputs['Trailing'] (inclusive — total including current), outputs['Total'] (full group sum, same on every element); CRITICAL: Leading/Total gives weighted-UV parameter in [0,1) where each element's UV interval width is proportional to its value — wide stripes for high-value elements, narrow for low; contrast with SplineParameter.Factor which gives uniform arc-length fractions independent of per-point values; Group ID = GeometryNodeInputSplineIndex.outputs[0] makes each spline accumulate independently — without this, multi-spline curves produce one continuous prefix sum across all splines and Total reflects the TOTAL of all splines; Sort Index can be a descending rank field to accumulate in non-natural order (e.g., largest face first for LOD prioritisation); Repeat Zone can also accumulate but evaluates body N×M times (O(N×M) vs O(M)) — use AccumulateField when possible; ShaderNodeTexNoise.noise_dimensions='1D' requires inputs['W'] socket (FLOAT); setting noise_dimensions='3D' exposes inputs['Vector'] instead — incompatible with FLOAT field from SplineParameter.Factor; ResampleCurve(mode='COUNT') gives equal arc-length spacing but SplineParameter.Factor is still 0..1 uniform; AccumulateField on noise weights gives NON-uniform UV (weighted arc-length); CurveToMesh transfers POINT-domain FLOAT_COLOR attributes to all mesh vertex rings at the corresponding curve-point position — each ring of profile vertices shares one colour; export_colors=True promotes FLOAT_COLOR → COLOR_0 glTF accessor; outside sources: Blender Manual Accumulate Field CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Documentation Team https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/utilities/field/accumulate_field.html related Field at Index Evaluate on Domain Spline Parameter; mode enum: 'ALL' (any two verts within threshold regardless of edge connectivity — REQUIRED after RealizeInstances because inter-instance seam verts share XYZ but have NO connecting edges, so CONNECTED skips them) vs 'CONNECTED' (only merges verts reachable via an edge chain — correct for closing sub-millimetre gaps within a single continuous shell without merging separate shells); outputs['Geometry'] (NodeSocketGeometry — merged result); PRIMARY PIPELINE: MeshGrid(vertices_x=GRID_COLS, vertices_y=GRID_ROWS, size_x=TILE_W*(COLS-1), size_y=TILE_D*(ROWS-1)) → MeshToPoints(mode='VERTS') → InstanceOnPoints → RealizeInstances → MergeByDistance(mode='ALL', Distance=WELD_DIST) → GroupOutput; WELD THRESHOLD FORMULA: WELD_DIST = min_intended_gap / 1000, safely above FP epsilon but below smallest feature; at 1m tile scale: 0.0005m = 0.5mm (400x margin below RIDGE_W=0.06m feature); MeshGrid spacing formula: spacing = size / (vertices - 1) → for TILE_W=1m spacing: size_x = TILE_W * (COLS-1); MeshToPoints(mode='VERTS') extracts exact grid vertex positions as point cloud — guarantees sub-millimetre alignment vs Distribute Points which randomises; after RealizeInstances: GRID_COLS*GRID_ROWS*verts_per_module total verts with seam positions coincident but topologically disconnected; post-weld count drops by merged seam pairs; DIAGNOSTIC: dg=bpy.context.evaluated_depsgraph_get(); ob_ev=ob.evaluated_get(dg); me_ev=ob_ev.to_mesh(); len(me_ev.vertices) → compare with pre-weld count; both PointsToVolume and VolumeToMesh must use SAME mode to avoid mismatched grid; ADAPTIVITY: post-MC decimation 0–1; near-coplanar triangles merge to fewer larger ones; 0.04–0.12 range halves poly count vs 0 while preserving organic silhouette; above 0.3 causes visible faceting at detail/flat transition — better to reduce VOXEL_AMOUNT instead; GN COMPOSABILITY vs Metaballs: Metaballs evaluated outside GN, cannot receive stored attributes, cannot be piped to downstream GN nodes; Points→Volume→Mesh pipeline fully composable — reconstructed mesh exits VolumeToMesh as ordinary geometry; WEBXR: VOXEL_AMOUNT=72 + Adaptivity=0.06 produces ~4000-6000 triangles; for background instances use VOXEL_AMOUNT=32 Adaptivity=0.15 = ~800 triangles; PROPERTY GROUP PREREQUISITE: raw IDProperties (obj['key']=val) have no RNA class-level descriptor so (Type,'prop') key is unavailable; must register bpy.types.PropertyGroup subclass via bpy.utils.register_class() and attach via PointerProperty for class-level subscription; fix: Mix Shader(Fac=Light Path.Is Shadow Ray, Shader1=PBSDF, Shader2=Transparent BSDF) — Fac=0 for camera rays uses PBSDF; Fac=1 for shadow rays uses Transparent which passes light through; EEVEE uses shadow_method='CLIP' directly (no node wiring); DOUBLE-SIDED FOLIAGE PATTERN: three crossed quad planes (billboard approximation) each with use_backface_culling=False provides convincing leaf volume from all viewing angles without a full 3D mesh; PROCEDURAL LEAF MASK: TexCoord(Object)→Mapping(scale X×1.2 Y×2.2)→Gradient(SPHERICAL)→ColorRamp(0.40–0.58) for oval body; Noise(scale=14,detail=6)→ColorRamp(0.35–0.55) for serrated edge; Math(MULTIPLY) combines both so pixel must pass both body AND serration test; threshold 0.42 sits just below 0.5 — raises to tighten serration, below 0.10 erodes body; RADIAL HOLO FADE: TexCoord(UV)→Gradient(SPHERICAL)→Math(SUBTRACT 1.0, fac)→ColorRamp(0.30–0.85) inverts gradient so centre=opaque, edge=transparent; SORTING ARTEFACT MITIGATION: keep BLEND planes non-overlapping in world space; for overlapping glass use OIT in engine (Babylon.js, Three.js depth-peeling); outside sources: KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 Khronos Group https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO writes alphaMode/alphaCutoff/doubleSided from Blender material properties; socket identifier is stable across renames (do NOT key by display name); update_tag() mandatory after modifier value changes — without it viewport does not redraw until next interactive event; frame stepper: scene.frame_set(n) + bpy.ops.screen.screenshot_area(filepath=...) per tick; outputs Points(Geometry) Normal(Vector=curve-frame Frenet normal) Tangent(Vector) Parameter(Float 0=start→1=end); Density mode: point count = density × arc_length so scatter adapts automatically to vine length changes; vine stem Branch A: CurvePrimitiveCircle(RADIUS,r=0.007,res=8) → CurveToMesh(FillCaps=True) → SetMaterial(mat_stem); leaf Branch B: DistributePointsOnCurves → MeshGrid(2×2 verts, Width=LeafW, Height=LeafH) → SetMaterial(mat_leaf) → FunctionNodeAlignEulerToVector(axis=Z,pivot=X) with Normal → FunctionNodeRotateEuler(AXIS_ANGLE,LOCAL) with random ±60° angle around Tangent axis → InstanceOnPoints(Scale=CombineXYZ(remap)) → RealizeInstances → JoinGeometry; RealizeInstances mandatory before GLB export (exporter cannot flatten nested instances); holoflow_macros/gn_distribute_points_on_curves_ivy.py provides build_ivy_scatter(curve_obj,...) reusable factory; outside sources: Blender Foundation Distribute Points on Curves Manual CC-BY-SA-4.0 https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/curve/operations/distribute_points_on_curves.html; | 2026-07-02 | blender-tutorial-rigging-vrm-eye-look-at-damped-track | VRM Eye Look-At Rig — Damped Track + Limit Rotation Bone Constraints (Blender 5.1) | blends/rigging/rigging-vrm-eye-look-at-damped-track/ | Damped Track vs Track To: Track To solves in Euler space and gimbal-locks at 90° pole causing eye-flip; Damped Track solves quaternion minimum-rotation (shortest path, always continuous) — preferred for real-time VRM avatars; track_axis='TRACK_NEGATIVE_Y' because Blender bones point +Y at rest and eye mesh faces -Y; self-targeting armature: constraint.target=arm_obj + constraint.subtarget='LookTarget' avoids extra Empty and keeps rig self-contained; constraint order is procedural: DampedTrack evaluates first (orient toward target), LimitRotation evaluates second (clamp result) — reversing order makes Limit fire on zero-rotation and Damped overwrites clamp entirely; Limit Rotation Space=POSE_SPACE: clamp follows head rotation (tilting head tilts the travel cone) — LOCAL_SPACE breaks when head is not at rest; use_transform_limit=True ensures clamp applies at all influence values not just 1.0; bilateral symmetry via Copy Rotation with invert_z=True: positive-Z yaw on LeftEye equals negative-Z yaw on RightEye because bones are mirrored across YZ plane — single checkbox, no extra Damped Track or math; RightEye constraint chain: CopyRotation(LeftEye, invertZ) → LimitRotation; LeftEye constraint chain: DampedTrack(LookTarget) → LimitRotation; LookTarget bone: child of Head (relative gaze), use_deform=False (control bone only), custom_shape=circle widget; VRM 1.0 LookAt spec: runtime reads leftEye/rightEye bone slots from VRMC_vrm extension and drives gaze by bone rotation — Limit Rotation in constraint chain clamps runtime drives automatically; GLB export: temp_override + export_scene.gltf, export_yup=True, export_apply=True, Draco level 6; outside sources: Blender Manual Damped Track CC-BY-SA-4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/5.1/animation/constraints/tracking/damped_track.html sibling https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/5.1/animation/constraints/index.html; | 2026-07-02 | blender-tutorial-shader-detail-normal-rnm-overlay-hard-surface | Shader — Reoriented Normal Mapping (RNM): Detail Normal Overlay on a Hard-Surface Panel (Blender 5.1) | blends/shading/shader-detail-normal-rnm-overlay-hard-surface/ | Problem: Mix Color lerp of two decoded normals is wrong — result shorter than 1, incorrect direction at 45°, visible highlight flattening; RNM formula (Ben Golus 2017): T=base Bump output, D=detail Bump output; Rx=Tx+Dx, Ry=Ty+Dy, Rz=Tz×Dz (multiply Z not add), R=normalize(Rx,Ry,Rz); Z-multiply preserves cosine relationship between normals, prevents over-brightening at perpendicular surfaces; six-node RNM chain: SeparateXYZ(T)+SeparateXYZ(D)→Math(ADD,Tx,Dx)→Math(ADD,Ty,Dy)→Math(MULTIPLY,Tz,Dz)→CombineXYZ→VectorMath(NORMALIZE)→PrincipledBSDF.Normal; base layer: Noise(Scale=2.5,Detail=3.0,Roughness=0.6)→Bump(Strength=0.8); detail layer: Voronoi(DISTANCE_TO_EDGE,Randomness=0,2D)→Math(SUBTRACT,1,dist)→Bump(Strength=0.35) = honeycomb rivet-ring pattern; glTF export limitation: glTF 2.0 normalTexture only accepts single image — bake RNM composite (Cycles Normal bake, Tangent space) before GLB export; Three.js TSL supports runtime RNM via NodeMaterial for WebXR; outside sources: Blender Manual Bump Node CC-BY Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/5.1/render/shader_nodes/vector/bump.html sibling blender/blender; Ben Golus "Normal Mapping for a Triplanar Shader" 2017 educational reference https://bgolus.medium.com/normal-mapping-for-a-triplanar-shader-10bf39dca05a sibling github.com/bgolus | 5.1 | CC0 |

inst.matrix_world must be deep-copied with .copy() — transient view invalidated on next loop iteration; writing captured positions to Named Attribute (FLOAT_VECTOR, POINT domain) on a one-vertex-per-particle mesh makes them accessible as mesh.userData extras in GLB; s.child_type check: child particles multiply s.count — set 'NONE' to disable; 5.1 migration: static scatter → GN Instance on Points, hair/fur → GN Curves Hair Curves, physics emitters → keep particle API;
