on_export(export_context) fires once after stage is filled, before disk flush; export_context.get_stage() returns pxr.Usd.Stage; on_material_export(export_context, bl_material, usd_material) fires once per material; pxr Python bindings (OpenUSD) are bundled in all official Blender 4.1+ builds — import inside try/except for safety; USDZ spec requires ZIP_STORED (no compression) — Quick Look uses byte-offset random access into the archive; compressed entries (.ZIP_DEFLATED) are rejected on device even if macOS Preview opens them; .usdc must be index 0 in the ZIP (first written entry); pxr.UsdUtils.CreateNewUsdzPackage(usdc_path, usdz_path) is the production packaging function — it rewrites internal texture paths to flat basenames automatically; | 2026-07-10 | blender-tutorial-python-gpu-offscreen-render-to-texture-webxr | Python gpu.types.GPUOffScreen — Offscreen Render-to-Texture: Custom GPU Bake for WebXR Data Textures (Blender 5.1) | blends/scripting/python-gpu-offscreen-render-to-texture-webxr/ | gpu.types.GPUOffScreen(width, height) allocates an OpenGL framebuffer — must call .free() explicitly; Python GC does NOT release VRAM; draw calls outside with offscreen.bind() context silently target the active viewport — no error, wrong output; gpu.state.active_framebuffer_get() returns the currently-bound framebuffer inside the with block; fb.clear(color=(...), depth=1.0) clears both colour and depth attachments — depth 1.0 = far plane; gpu.state.depth_test_set() and depth_mask_set() must be called INSIDE the with block — gpu.state is framebuffer-scoped in 5.1; fb.read_color(x, y, w, h, channels, slot, dtype) — dtype="FLOAT" returns float32 [0,1]; channels=4 for RGBA; slot=0 for colour attachment 0; offscreen.texture_color returns GPUTexture not pixels — read via fb.read_color() not direct texture access; GPUShader(vert_src, frag_src) raises RuntimeError on GLSL error with log attached; per-loop vertex buffer required for flat-shaded meshes — per-vertex buffer cannot represent discontinuous normals at shared edges; normal matrix = inverse-transpose of 3x3 model sub-matrix — required for non-uniform scale; mathutils.Matrix.OrthoProjection() does not accept frustum boundaries (l,r,b,t) — build the matrix manually for full control; ortho bake avoids perspective foreshortening artefacts at UV edges; remap normal [-1,1]→[0,1] for unsigned texture storage: n = normal * 0.5 + 0.5; depth in alpha avoids a second pass — unpack in TSL with texture.a; set scene.view_settings.view_transform = "Raw" before save_render() to export linear data without tone-mapping; img.pixels[:] row-major bottom-up matches OpenGL convention; float_buffer=True preserves 32-bit precision in memory; save_render() goes through colour management pipeline unlike img.save(); batch_for_shader helper attribute name must exactly match GLSL in attribute name; gpu_extras.batch.batch_for_shader() is the 5.1-recommended helper replacing manual GPUVertFormat boilerplate; | 2026-07-10 | blender-tutorial-python-mesh-custom-split-normals-smooth-island-faceted-webxr | Python bpy.types.Mesh — Custom Split Normals: Per-Loop Normal Authoring, Smooth Shading Islands & Faceted GLB for WebXR (Blender 5.1) | blends/scripting/python-mesh-custom-split-normals-smooth-island-faceted-webxr/ | mesh.use_auto_smooth and mesh.auto_smooth_angle were removed in Blender 4.1 — accessing either raises AttributeError in 5.1; replacement is "Smooth by Angle" GN modifier added via bpy.ops.object.shade_smooth_by_angle() but that modifier OVERRIDES normals_split_custom_set() — never add it to a mesh using custom split normals; normals_split_custom_set(normals) requires list length == len(mesh.loops) == sum(p.loop_total for p in mesh.polygons) — mismatch raises RuntimeError; for flat-shaded polygons (poly.use_smooth=False) the custom normal is silently ignored — always set use_smooth=True before calling normals_split_custom_set(); loop ordering: for each polygon in mesh.polygons order, its loops are [poly.loop_start, poly.loop_start+poly.loop_total) — the per-loop list must follow this exact traversal; calc_normals_split() evaluates split normals into mesh.loops[i].normal (read-only temporary); free_normals_split() releases the temporary; never call calc_normals() in 5.1 — function removed in 4.1; bpy.ops.object.transform_apply() clears custom split normals — always apply_custom_normals AFTER applying transforms; export_apply=False in the GLTF exporter is required when the object has no modifiers; with export_apply=True a "Smooth by Angle" modifier present would recompute normals overwriting custom values; export_normals=True (default) tells GLTF exporter to emit NORMAL attribute; exporter calls calc_normals_split() on evaluated mesh and reads loop normals including custom data; Union-Find path compression (parent[x]=parent[parent[x]]) is critical for transitivity — without it A≈B and B≈C may not correctly merge A and C; area-weighted normal averaging prevents tiny boundary slivers from pulling island normals off-axis; bmesh.ops.create_icosphere(bm, subdivisions=2, radius=1.0) needs no active context unlike bpy.ops.mesh.primitive_ico_sphere_add(); mesh.update(calc_edges=True) refreshes geometry and face normals in 4.1+; normals_split_custom_set_from_vertices(normals) is the per-vertex variant — slower, length=len(mesh.vertices); outside sources: Blender Foundation bpy.types.Mesh API CC-BY-SA-4.0 https://docs.blender.org/api/5.1/bpy.types.Mesh.html sibling bpy.types.MeshLoop https://docs.blender.org/api/5.1/bpy.types.MeshLoop.html; bpy.types.LayerCollection is the per-ViewLayer *wrapper* in scene.view_layers[n].layer_collection tree — they are DIFFERENT objects; setting collection.hide_render on the data block has NO effect at render time — all render visibility flags live on LayerCollection; scene.collection (the master root) is NOT in bpy.data.collections — querying bpy.data.collections['Scene'] raises KeyError in 5.1; find_layer_collection(root_lc, name) depth-first traversal is the only way to locate a LayerCollection by name — no direct hash map exists; LayerCollection.exclude=True removes the collection from the view layer entirely — objects not evaluated, no shadows, no GI; LayerCollection.hide_viewport hides in viewport only — objects still render; LayerCollection.holdout renders as alpha compositing cut-out; LayerCollection.indirect_only: collection contributes to GI/shadows but does not appear in direct render output — correct flag for light collections in baked-lighting WebXR pipelines; LayerCollection flags are NOT keyframeable via insert_keyframe() — animate by rendering frame segments with flags toggled between bpy.ops.render.render() calls; collection.objects is NOT recursive — child collection objects excluded; for recursive gather, walk collection.children tree; add_to_collection pattern: iterate obj.users_collection and unlink from all, then col.objects.link(obj) — avoids duplicate-collection membership artefacts; new objects from bpy.ops land in the ACTIVE collection, not the target collection — always re-link manually; make_collection: bpy.data.collections.new() + parent_col.children.link(col) — bare new() is invisible until linked; batch export: select only col.objects members, bpy.ops.export_scene.gltf(use_selection=True) — produces independently loadable GLBs for per-chunk WebXR streaming; export_lights=True safe to leave on globally — exporter silently skips non-light objects from selection;
